2026-08-08 — M5 / T16 completion addendum: Git is a host capability, not an agent tool

The boundary is intentionally drawn at command execution, not at a convention in a prompt. The new modules do not import node:child_process; they use the existing runSystemCommand() capability in transport-broker.ts, preserving the repository-wide one-import fence. That matters because a second Git-spawning convenience function would become a second route around the host's process ownership boundary. Every invocation is an executable-plus-argv call to git; no shell is involved.

A managed tree is named by the attempt, and Q8 remains a machine decision. createWorktree() accepts the canonical repository, an absolute machine-local root, one path-component attempt id, and a base revision. It resolves the base to a SHA before asking Git for a detached worktree at <root>/<attemptId>. Traversal and a relative root are refused before Git runs. listWorktrees() parses Git's porcelain listing and returns only entries contained by that configured root. There is intentionally no counterpart that removes or prunes a worktree: the source-level destructive-paths test now makes either Git subcommand a failing repository condition, rather than relying on the absence to remain accidental.

The fingerprint compares states, not merely an after-diff. captureChangeSet() records each tracked path from git diff HEAD --numstat as its added/deleted count and each non-ignored untracked path as untracked. changedPaths(before, after) takes the union of paths and compares values. Consequently a path that was dirty before a phase and absent afterward is still reported: restoring a file with git checkout is a mutation even though it leaves no line in the final diff. The same union rule covers an appearing untracked file, a deletion, and a changed numstat. Gitignored session-runtime material is absent by Git's own exclusion rule rather than by an AWSF special case.

Freshness is a separate assertion because a green result describes one tree, not all future trees. A gate records the exact fingerprint it observed. invalidateStaleResults() compares a later snapshot with that record and clears both gatesCurrent and reviewCurrent on any difference; a review cannot remain authoritative after its candidate has changed. withCleanWorktree() checks porcelain cleanliness immediately before and after a gate, so a gate cannot quietly judge one tree while leaving another for its successor. The host commit path similarly refuses an empty candidate, stages the complete captured change-set, fixes both Git's author and committer identity to the owner, and requires cleanliness after commit.

The tests name the failure modes rather than only constructors. The T16 unit suites cover tracked numstat plus untracked capture; appearances, rewrites and removals; the prior-dirty-path reversion; gate-before and gate-after dirtiness; post-gate invalidation of both gates and review; root containment and one-attempt worktree naming; root-filtered listing; and the exact host identity arguments. The strengthened meta-test protects the permanent non-removal rule. After the documentation commit, npm test passed: 671 unit tests, 45 contract tests, 49 simulation tests, and no journey tests yet; npm run lint passed with zero warnings. npm run typecheck remains the repository-wide missing-Node-typings failure first recorded in T1, not a T16 claim of success. No worktree was created, removed, or pruned to prove this task; no live provider call was spent. built on: gpt-5.6-terra · medium.

2026-08-08 — Milestone 5 / Task 17 complete: permission breaches abort; gates correct

The seven layers are one host-owned lifecycle, not seven claims in a prompt. PermissionSession validates that the canonical checkout, managed worktree, and always-writable session runtime are disjoint; requires a clean worktree before the run; resolves the exact configured tool allowlist inside a fail-closed profile ceiling; captures the pre-run fingerprint; prepares the OS grant; compares the complete post-run change-set; and enforces normalized repository-relative write and protected-path globs. The two real adapters now narrow their provider-specific tool flags from that exact configured list (exec maps to Bash/bash; find/ls map to Claude's Glob) rather than treating a broad profile as the final allowlist.

A breach is intentionally missing a correction API. PermissionBreach carries correctable: false, the phase cause permission-breach, and the sorted complete set of offending paths. PermissionSession.enforce() has no callback through which a workflow could spend a correction allowance. The zero-spend journey creates a real detached Git worktree, runs a scripted stub that writes one allowed file, two offending repository files, and a report under the session runtime, then proves one terminal breach names both repository paths and makes zero correction attempts. The reviewer case separately proves that writes: [] rejects a quiet repository edit while an unchanged review remains green; session-runtime output stays writable in both cases.

Path matching fails closed on ambiguous spellings. A single * cannot cross /; globstar can. Absolute paths, traversal, dot/empty segments, backslashes, control characters, malformed globs, and drive-shaped paths are refused rather than normalized into a different authorization. Protected paths are checked independently of write grants and case-insensitively on the platform that needs it. Git fingerprints now use NUL-framed --numstat --no-renames and NUL-framed untracked output, so tabs, newlines, quotes, and non-ASCII filenames reach policy as Git observed them; rename sides are explicit rather than collapsed into display syntax.

The sandbox badge says exactly what was established. On Linux with an available bwrap, the broker emits a shell-free descriptor with a read-only host root, explicit read-only canonical checkout, writable attempt runtime, and either a writable managed worktree for write-capable phases or a read-only one for writes: []; provider scratch is redirected into the attempt runtime. Without bwrap Linux reports tool-policy; Darwin remains unavailable until its own machine supplies and verifies a port. This development machine has no bwrap, so the descriptor and tri-state are unit-proven and no live Linux or Darwin platform claim is made; T27 still owns per-machine verification.

Risk and redaction share the same fail-closed bias. risk.paths selects the highest matched tier over the configured default; only an explicit operator override with a non-blank reason can replace it. One shared credential scrubber now owns the pattern vocabulary used by config loading, effective settings, provider environment filtering, canonical journal writes, status/continuity writes, and SQLite projection. Journal, status, and database tests prove shaped values are absent after each boundary; the fixture sweep imports the same vocabulary, and a meta-test prevents current persistence/display/provider boundaries from growing private copies. Private raw provider streams remain the plan's deliberately unprojected capture boundary and are never served as API data.

2026-08-08 — Milestone 5 / Task 18 complete: eleven evidence-bearing gates

A green gate now says what it proved. GateReport.check() appends immutable evidence for both passing and failing checks, returns the same report for deliberate chaining, and derives its verdict from the complete check list rather than from a caller-supplied boolean. Every gate walks all applicable items instead of returning at the first failure. Even an empty applicable set receives an explicit check, so a green report can never have an empty explanation. The seven modules under core/src/gates/ implement the complete eleven-gate table without introducing another command or filesystem execution route.

Artifact and envelope gates consume host observations rather than reaching around the host. envelope_valid records schema validity and producer success separately, including both checks when parsing failed. artifacts_exist records every declared path and observed byte size; files_non_empty distinguishes missing from zero-byte files; and json_parses records the parsed top-level type. Artifact access is injected as observations, so T19's phase engine remains the owner of containment-checked filesystem reads and these gates stay deterministic under unit tests.

The named silent diff failures are closed in both directions. diff_matches_claims compares unique, sorted host-captured and producer-claimed path sets exactly: an invented claim fails, an undeclared host change fails, ordering is irrelevant, and a deletion remains an ordinary member of the host set rather than disappearing from evidence. head_advanced separately requires both the existence of a host commit and a HEAD SHA different from base, closing the success-with-no-diff path. no_protected_paths and writes_within_globs record every changed path; invalid path or policy spellings fail closed instead of being repaired into a different authorization.

Execution evidence is pinned to the candidate it describes. commands_pass records the configured gate id, exact argv, exact candidate SHA, clean-before and clean-after observations, exit zero, and the bounded output tail without parsing runner-specific failure text. verdict_consistent records the reviewed SHA, candidate-context finding paths, concrete concern evidence, and the rule that an accept verdict cannot carry a high or critical finding. journey_passes keeps “ran”, “passed”, and “exact candidate SHA” as separate checks, so a stale successful journey cannot authorize a newer tree.

Coverage, results, and the boundary left open. Eleven gate suites each carry a positive and negative case, plus a direct GateReport evidence test: the focused suite is 23/23 green. The signature negatives explicitly prove the undeclared diff, unchanged HEAD, protected-path touch, and accept-with-critical-finding cases. The full npm run test:unit suite is 725/725 green, and npm run lint is clean with zero warnings. 2026-08-08 — Milestone 5 / Task 19 complete: corrections preserve context and calls

The compiler now rejects bad intent before execution. compilePhase() applies the earned-description rule to blanks and normalized name restatements, verifies that each schema id and TypeBox object are the one registered source, and injects the emitted JSON Schema into every agent prompt. A downstream phase that receives an envelope but omits {previous_envelope} fails rather than quietly discarding its handoff. compileWorkflow() rejects duplicate phase ids and calls the existing workflow-fit budget hook before any provider can run.

Success and correction continuity are structural. A phase emits QUEUED first, reaches SUCCEEDED only from validation, and records FAILED on every abnormal exit. The engine accepts one already-open CorrectionSession with no reopen, factory, fallback, or substitution method; every returned turn is checked against the original adapter, provider, resolved model, and provider session id. A correction transport failure is terminal and explicitly records that no cold restart was attempted. The real adapters continue to advertise continuity: none until their provider-specific multi-turn transports are separately verified; this task proves the engine seam without inventing live-provider behavior from a stub.

The complete loop preserves evidence and ordering. Each send is parsed against the registered envelope schema; at most two JSON-fix turns are allowed, and every invalid result is persisted with its exact bounded violations and raw-output reference. Gate failures produce a host-rendered correction request carrying phase and round, the prior envelope id, schema violations, every gate check with a 4,000-character tail, and the live remaining call budget. Gate and schema corrections draw the existing per-phase tranches but never reserve or spend a tier call. Only after a clean gate round does the engine call permissions.enforce(); a breach aborts before diff capture or commit. The production Git boundary then captures the host-observed change set and commits it through T16's owner-attributed host path.

Accounting separates cumulative spend from occupancy. Token counts and available cost accumulate over every turn, while context occupancy is exactly the last turn's report. The terminal agent-session persistence callback receives those phase totals and the host candidate SHA. Unit coverage proves compile-time rejection, generated-schema and previous-envelope rendering, success-must-be-earned, retained invalid envelopes, the two-fix ceiling, bounded correction evidence, all four identity fields, permission-after-loop ordering, host diff/commit ordering, call neutrality, cumulative usage, and no cold restart after transport failure.

2026-08-08 — Milestone 6 / Task 21 complete: one human gate, persisted before Git moves

The CLI is one journal-backed command surface. core/src/cli/main.ts, tty.ts, and the command modules implement new, start, status, watch, land, cancel, and retry; npm run awsf -- … is the strip-only entry point. The CLI attempt record carries lifecycle, phase/round, task-lifetime call and correction meters, resolved model plus provenance, exact candidate, process identity, last activity, blocker and next action through the existing append+fsync / atomic-status write protocol. Sealed attempts reject any further write. new mints DRAFT; start validates config and preflights, pins HEAD and creates the detached attempt tree before L1 PREPARED; cancel runs the shared TERM/KILL/enumerate controller and names survivors rather than claiming an empty tree; retry creates attempt n+1 at DRAFT, with task-lifetime calls carried and no transition out of the sealed attempt.

L20 has one call site, and its order is the rejection contract's order. authorizeLanding() in commands/land.ts is the sole production request whose target is LANDING. It delegates actor, medium, and evidence to transition() rather than pre-rejecting them in CLI convenience code: host and owner therefore hear step 7 ActorNotPermitted; a human on piped stdin hears step 8 InteractiveOwnerRequired; only then can step 10 critique confirmation or Git evidence. The real terminal implementation contains the literal !process.stdin.isTTY refusal. No API module imports either landing boundary, and the journey grep asserts both the one-site and no-route properties.

The SHA a human approves and the SHA Git receives are the same bytes. The command resolves the recorded 40-hex commit, prints that exact SHA, commit subject, diff stat, and ahead/behind meter, then asks one yes/no question. L20 and the approval summary are appended and the LANDING status is atomically persisted before any Git mutation. The read-only fast-forward inspection required by L20 is repeated after persistence to close the confirmation-to-mutation race; only a local merge --ff-only is available. L23 is persisted only after canonical HEAD is re-read equal to the candidate and status --porcelain is empty. A race or recovered landing that is dirty or divergent takes L24 with dirty-canonical-tree or non-fast-forward and exact ahead/behind counts; unreadable recovery takes ambiguous-recovery. An initial failed preflight remains at AWAITING_OWNER, as L20's normative guard requires; L24 applies only once a previously green preflight has been durably approved and then changed or crashed.

Crash recovery does not ask twice and does not guess. A durable LANDING record is itself the exact approval, so rerunning awsf land does not require another TTY: if canonical HEAD is already the candidate and clean, L23 records it; if the tree is still clean and the candidate is still a descendant, the one approved fast-forward is resumed; dirty, divergent, or unreadable reality blocks. Simulation injects the crash immediately after durable L20 and covers resume, 1-ahead/1-behind divergence, dirty checkout, and unreadable Git state.

2026-08-09 — M6 / Task 22 complete: operator controls and the missing fixture execution seam

The friction was a real contradiction between the checklist and the command surface, not a missing test flag. M6 said to feel a simple-sdlc run from start through land. T21 deliberately made awsf start stop after the durable L1 worktree record at PREPARED; that is correct — launching a provider is L4, not an incidental side effect of materialising a worktree. But no subsequent owner command existed to exercise even the committed fixture path, so the written sequence could never produce a candidate or reach AWAITING_OWNER. The first attempted terminal proof exposed this immediately: there was no state-changing command between start and land capable of earning the latter's evidence.

The owner-approved repair is deliberately narrow. awsf run <task> --stub true is available only for simple-sdlc, only after L1, and only with an explicit fixture flag; start --stub true likewise attests the fixture preflight rather than claiming a configured production adapter was available. It writes one deterministic file under .awsf-stub/ in the detached worktree, makes the host-owned candidate commit, and carries the attempt through L4/L7/L11/L15 to the existing interactive L20 boundary. It invokes no provider and spends no subscription quota. This is a terminal demonstration seam, not a fallback route, a model adapter, or a claim that fixture output substitutes for the workflow engine's real agent/host execution; those remain distinct production boundaries.

The manual run found one more operational condition worth preserving. Landing initially refused with a failed fast-forward preflight, not because the candidate was divergent, but because placing a temporary awsf.config.yaml inside the canonical test repository made that checkout dirty. Re-running with the test configuration outside the canonical checkout produced the intended proof: a pseudo-TTY answered the one owner prompt, local fast-forward landed test: complete stub simple-sdlc, and canonical status was clean. That is exactly the distinction L20 is meant to protect: an unclean canonical checkout is never waved through merely because the candidate itself looks valid.

Operator controls stay read-only wherever their names imply diagnosis. doctor reads attempt locks, recorded PIDs, degraded projection rows, and the lifecycle matrix without taking a lock or writing state; healthy exits 0 and findings exit 1. Its simulation injects a live PID into a terminal attempt and proves that the finding appears while both journal and status bytes remain identical. db rebuild supplies the CLI attempt identity T8 intentionally left to its caller and preserves its candidate/retained-file protocol. gc names terminal attempts and retained projections but exposes no deletion path. dash serves an already-built dashboard or says plainly that there is no build; it never builds one as a side effect.

Verification and marker decision. The final component runs are green: 742 unit, 45 contract, 54 simulation, and 10 journey tests; lint is clean. The ticket's frontmatter is state: done in this same change. The original checklist wording is amended above to name the indispensable fixture execution command, and after the successful terminal landing T22, every M6 checklist row, and the M6 header are earned. built on: pi.

2026-08-09 — Milestone 7 / Task 23 complete: the dashboard gets one narrow door

The route table is data, and its absence is now mechanically meaningful. core/src/api/routes.ts exports exactly seven GET declarations and one POST declaration: health, sessions, one session, one phase, cursor events, settings, adapters, and archive. no-write-route.test.ts compares that table byte-for-route against the allowlist and fails on any extra verb or lifecycle/configuration mutation name; the older repository-wide no-land-route fence remains independently green. Archive delegates its one UPDATE to the existing SQLite write boundary and opens a separate write connection only on the first archive request. The read connection is opened eagerly with {readonly: true}, and a route test records both opens to prove their order and modes rather than trusting comments.

Loopback is a runtime assertion, not a TypeScript suggestion. server.ts accepts only literal 127.0.0.1 or ::1; security.ts rejects non-loopback Host authorities, requires an optional Origin to match the Host exactly over HTTP, decodes each path segment once, and refuses every segment outside /^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$/ including empty, dot, slash-bearing, backslash-bearing, and malformed-percent forms. Every declared handler is constructed through safely(), and dispatch has a second error boundary around authority, path, and route matching. Every JSON response carries default-src 'none', self-only script/style/font/connect directives, frame/form/object prohibitions, no-store/nosniff headers, and no CORS grant; this server exposes no asset route, remote or otherwise.

The response boundary is explicit-column and deny-by-shape. observability/queries.ts names public columns rather than selecting session, agent, process, gate, envelope, or event records wholesale. Host continuity references, journal/envelope/output paths, process argv/cwd/identity, and private-ref event payloads never enter a public DTO; responses.ts recursively removes continuity-reference and raw-log/output/stream keys in snake, kebab, or camel case and then runs the shared credential scrubber as defense in depth. Sessions embed ordered phases and agents; event pages use event_row insertion order with strict nonnegative after, a 1..500 limit, one-row lookahead for hasMore, and the last returned row as cursor. The phase DTO reserves compiledPrompts but returns an empty list today: migration 0001 projects no compiled-prompt content, and a readonly API must not follow private file references to manufacture it.

The visual contract was carried into one small shell, rather than approximated with a generic dashboard. dashboard/src/styles/dashboard.css declares the contract's dark background, translucent and solid surfaces, border/text/muted palette, all seven agent fallback colours, state colours, Inter/Outfit and JetBrains/Fira font stacks, tabular figures, radii, and motion tokens verbatim. The timing policy has one owner. usePolling chooses 500 ms while health reports active sessions, 2 s for a populated inactive grid, and 5 s for an empty view. A rejected fetch increments an exponential delay, capped at 30 seconds; a successful fetch alone stamps lastPollAt and clears that penalty. Regaining browser focus asks for a poll immediately. LiveIndicator is green only where that successful poll is younger than two times the selected cadence, so a request that failed or never completed cannot borrow the appearance of live data.

2026-08-10 — Out-of-band M8 contract repair checkpoint 1: task edges and phase launches are separate authorizations

The contradiction was real and the owner approved this repair out of band. The lifecycle matrix intentionally has five task-edge launch sites, while the Phase Contract intentionally permits workflows with several sequential agent phases. After L4, the durable task is already RUNNING; pretending each later phase is another PREPARED → RUNNING registration would falsify persisted history, and calling transition(RUNNING, RUNNING) would correctly raise AlreadyInState. Keeping one provider process and swapping provider/model/prompt/tools/permissions inside it was rejected too: those are process configuration and one process cannot honestly be several separately configured calls. A caller-invented bearer string was also rejected because it would move the trust decision into the registration it is meant to distrust.

The chosen split leaves the task contract byte-for-behavior intact. ProcessRegistration remains the strict task-edge form and still derives exactly L4/L10/L11/L16/L19 from LEGAL_EDGES. A second discriminant, kind: "agent-phase", carries task session, workflow, phase id, one-based ordinal, adapter, role, run, and reservation. Its separate validator runs before child creation and asks an injected trusted-host verifier to prove durable RUNNING, exact membership in the retained compiled workflow, and the explicit configured route; the broker then cross-checks that evidence and exactly one still-held call reservation. No verifier means no phase launch. This keeps execution independent of status-store I/O and cycles: host composition is trusted and launch callers are not. Mandatory review and first owner-rework launches remain task-edge-only by explicit route authorization. LEGAL_EDGES, all 24 legal and 76 rejected pairs, the eleven-step rejection order, CORRECTION_EDGES, and RUNNING → RUNNING were not changed.

No-process-before-registration is proved on both sides. The real sentinel sweep rejects wrong durable state, unknown phase, engineer and code phases, mismatched workflow/phase identity, absent/unknown reservation, malformed ordinal, provider selected outside config, REVIEWING, task-edge-only review/rework, absent verifier, and verifier equivocation, then proves its launcher's marker file does not exist and the reservation remains held. The positive simulation starts the second agent phase of compiled plan-build-test as a real stub process after one L4 spend, records edge: null plus exact phase identity, and proves the second reservation becomes spent on GO. This checkpoint deliberately does not build the production workflow runner that will compose these contracts.

The human-gate regression belonged to the fixture's projection lifecycle, not to the hold. Before editing, the focused journey reproduced the clean baseline at 9/10 pass: the healthy landing case failed with DegradedObservabilityHold. Its seed wrote journal/status directly and never projected revision 1; when the real CLI opened a fresh database, the missing session correctly read degraded. The fixture now seeds through the production dashboard projector. The healthy case lands, while a strengthened negative deliberately skips projection revision 2 and projects revision 3, producing a real non-contiguous projection failure; that case remains AWAITING_OWNER, leaves canonical HEAD unchanged, and writes no LANDING record. assertAdvancementPermitted(), fatal projection handling at protected states, and the degraded hold itself were not bypassed or weakened. A 20-second child-start timeout also expired once under the first full parallel verification; it was widened to 60 seconds because the test proves piped-stdin refusal rather than startup latency, and the final run remained under 19 seconds.

Verification and scope boundary. The focused lifecycle/phase/barrier/workflow set is 160/160 green; focused human gate is 7/7; focused ticket-plan synchronization is 9/9. Final npm test is green at 777/777 unit, 45/45 contract, 55/55 simulation, and 11/11 journey. npm run lint is clean with zero warnings and git diff --check is clean. Typecheck remains the documented missing-Node-declarations baseline: 651 → 666 diagnostics, with no new diagnostic code/class and all fifteen additions confined to new Node-importing test files; core/src/workflow/phase-launch-authorization.ts has no diagnostic. No live provider ran, no pilot state was encoded, and T29, its ticket, and the M8 marker remain unchanged.

2026-08-11 — Out-of-band M8 contract repair checkpoint 2: the real T1 production workflow runner

The production command is now distinct from both preparation and demonstration. awsf start still stops at durable L1 PREPARED. awsf run TASK --stub true is still the explicit simple-sdlc fixture path. Without that flag, awsf run TASK compiles and drives only the completely bound T1 build and plan-build-test recipes; every other recipe fails before lifecycle mutation or provider launch. This checkpoint does not wire T2 review and does not perform or record either pilot.

The failed independent verification found a real continuity defect, now repaired. The runner still resolves each role only from its configured agent, adapter, model, thinking level, prompt files, tool profile, and writes, but it now maps the adapter's verified ModelInfo.continuity capability to the durable agents[].harness.continuity intent and requires an exact match before broker construction or L4. Either-direction mismatch blocks with zero calls and no process evidence; even matching same-session declarations remain blocked because this runner has no verified correction transport. The committed planner, builder, and documenter routes now truthfully declare none, matching both real adapters; no multi-turn support was invented. With that admission passed, task-edge L4 and later host-verified compiled-phase launches retain one reservation per provider and a malformed envelope or failed phase gate blocks after one send.

Exact process evidence is intentionally machine-local, not public. The normative ownership and DDL are awsf-architecture-proposal.md §7.2 and §7.8: canonical journals and SQLite are machine-local lifecycle state, while processes.command_json and cwd_display are required audit columns. They therefore retain the resolved executable, exact argv — including a private system-prompt path where the reviewed CLI requires it — and managed cwd; prompt content remains outside argv. The §7.9 API contract returns only an explicit process summary. processesForSession() selects neither audit column, and negative query plus session/phase API tests seed absolute private paths and prove neither field nor path crosses the read boundary. Raw output, envelope, and gate references remain attempt-relative.

The proof now crosses the real production process boundary. Fast injected orchestration cases remain, but the focused suite's new positive uses the real ProcessTransportBroker, gated launcher, executable resolution, spend-on-GO ledger, and the real pi captured-stream parser against a zero-quota executable fixture whose line shapes already exist in the captured corpus. At provider start the fixture reads the durable journal and proves both REGISTERED and spent/RUNNING process evidence predate GO; the final trace has contiguous revisions, one provider usage report counted once, an exited process, exact candidate and gates, dashboard visibility, no private API path, no prompt content in argv, and no process or reservation residue. A process-backed parser-failure negative proves the already-spent call remains billed while held reservations return to zero. Continuity mismatch and unsupported matching multi-turn intent join unsupported workflow, unavailable adapter, registration, malformed-envelope, permission, gate, configured-command, and projector-degradation negatives. Focused production coverage is 15/15.

2026-08-11 — Out-of-band M8 contract repair checkpoint 3: production-runner evidence closes before Pilot 1

2026-08-12 — Agent-sandbox survey: the isolation boundary AWSF already owns, and the one it deliberately does not

What was surveyed, and how the evidence was separated. A fifth external system — inkwell-agent-sandboxes-and-software-factory, read strictly read-only at pinned commit 92f1701, never installed and never executed — was compared against this repository's sandbox, permission, process, environment, portability, observability, and owner-control contracts at current HEAD. Four evidence classes were kept apart and are labelled as such below: transcript claims (the narrated video), screenshot observations (39 captures), public-reference claims (exe.dev's durable-sandbox positioning and OpenRouter's provisioning-key API), and source-verified behaviour (a cited file and line in the clone). Only the fourth class was allowed to decide anything. Following the precedent set by the earlier work-intake survey, a surveyed-but-largely-unadopted repository is recorded here and not added to the Current-Source Audit table, which audits the three repositories this system actually fuses.

Every mechanism the source enforces in code, AWSF already enforces — in four of five cases more strictly. Post-hoc change-set permission enforcement: adws/adw_modules/permissions.py fingerprints the working tree before a phase and diffs it after, precisely because "tools: is a capability list, not a sandbox" — AWSF reached the same conclusion independently in T17, and PermissionSession (core/src/policy/sandbox-broker.ts:164-188) additionally asserts the worktree is clean before capture, so a pre-dirty tree is refused rather than tolerated. Environment filtering: the source keeps its provisioning secret host-side and writes only a disposable runtime key into the guest; AWSF hands a provider child a three-name allowlist plus five injected values and raises E_REDACTION if even an allowlisted name holds credential-shaped bytes (core/src/adapters/env.ts:29-69). Process registration and cancellation: just/obs.just:47-66 records live pids in a table and guards the kill with a ps command-substring check against pid reuse; AWSF carries <boot-id>:<pid>:<starttime> across execve (core/src/execution/launcher.ts:61-89) and refuses to signal at all when identity is unverifiable or changed (core/src/execution/process-controller.ts:322-344) — a substring heuristic is not an identity. The zero-cost trap: assertion D of the source's five-assertion setup gate (just/sandbox/lifecycle/setup.just:205-243) exists because a partial rate table makes the harness report $0.0000 while genuinely spending; AWSF's cost-authority contract already renders the authority beside the figure, and T14's cross-building Amendment recorded exactly this class of correction. Cross-process run state: sandbox_mount/host/run_record.py is the source's single shared handle; AWSF's journal and status store are that, durably and with replay. None of these earns a task.

Rejected, with reasons, so they are not re-proposed. Ephemeral remote VMs as the isolation boundary (source-verified: the six phases under just/sandbox/lifecycle/) — it buys host-independent isolation by requiring an account, ssh reachability, and a network hop for every run, against a plan whose Explicitly Not Built line is "the factory cannot even push to the internet". AWSF's boundary is bwrap over a managed worktree with the canonical checkout --ro-bind-mounted (core/src/policy/sandbox-broker.ts:77-104), and its honesty mechanism is the tri-state badge rather than a claim. Minting a scoped API key per run with a spend cap and revoking it at teardown (source-verified at just/sandbox/lifecycle/create.just:127-169; public-reference claim: OpenRouter's management-key API supports exactly this) — a genuinely good design for metered API billing, and inapplicable here: AWSF routes through owner subscriptions, holds no credential to mint or revoke, and bounds work by tier call ceilings and the reservation ledger, which is a cap on calls rather than dollars. Adopting it would mean introducing the first credential this repository has ever held, against invariant 9. Full permission bypass justified by the box (source-verified in five places, e.g. just/sandbox/run/mod.just:86; transcript claim: "the blast radius is the box") — AWSF keeps both layers and never passes a bypass flag: profiles narrow via --permission-mode with an explicit allow list and an explicit deny list (core/src/adapters/claude-code.ts:145-182), so a failed OS sandbox degrades to tool-policy instead of to nothing. Publicly exposing the observability UI (screenshot observation: IMG_3383 shows the visualizer served from redesign-frontier-…exe.xyz:4600) — the AWSF dashboard is a machine-local read-only glass wall with no write route. Best-of-N fan-out across five sandboxes (transcript claim, corroborated by screenshots) — one operator, one project, and T30 is already the recorded cross-provider experiment. Rollback-on-breach with a three-slip forgiveness budget (permissions.py, RECOVERED_LIMIT = 3) — that module rolls back because its agent writes directly into the operator's repository; AWSF's worker writes into a disposable worktree that only an owner at a TTY can land, so a breach is terminal by construction (core/src/policy/path-policy.ts:144-164) and the offending tree is retained as evidence. Undoing it would destroy the evidence.

Two convergences and one limitation, recorded rather than acted on. The source independently arrived at two rules this plan already holds: a failing gate never destroys the box, because "a gate that auto-destroys throws away the evidence" (setup.just:47-58) — AWSF retains failed candidates and awsf gc lists without deleting; and work returns from the box as a bundle into refs/sandbox/ rather than a push, explicitly so the autonomous agent never holds a credential that could write to the repository it was cloned from (just/sandbox/manage/harvest.just:6-12) — AWSF needs no return trip at all, since the worktree is already machine-local and landing is a local fast-forward. The limitation the comparison sharpens is one already recorded and already blocked on hardware: the source is OS-agnostic inside the guest, whereas grantSandbox yields os-enforced only on Linux with bwrap present and reports unavailable on darwin and win32 rather than guessing. T27's macOS cells remain PENDING until the machine exists, and a Seatbelt port is noted at the call site; no new task is opened for a port that cannot be verified from this machine, because filling a platform cell from another platform is the one thing G7 forbids.

Conclusion: no net-new task is earned, and none was invented. After checking current HEAD rather than inferring from the earlier plan text, every source-verified mechanism is either already covered — usually more strictly — or rejected above on a stated architectural ground. No milestone, task, ticket, checklist marker, or acceptance row was added or changed; the dashboard-parity and pilot-runtime repairs already on HEAD were not re-proposed. This is a specs-only survey record: T29 remains todo with its four checklist boxes unchecked, M8 remains [wip], and no pilot run, candidate landing, pilot record, or completion metadata is claimed by it. built on: claude:opus-5.
